<p><b>Rajivaratna Gandhi</b><sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="/dʒ/: &#39;j&#39; in &#39;jam&#39;">dʒ</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span></span><span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/d\/d7\/Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg\/Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/d7/Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg/Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg" title="File:Hi-RajivGandhi.ogg">i</a></sup></span>; 20 August 1944 – 21 May 1991)<sup id="cite_ref-brit_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> was an Indian politician who served as the <a href="/wiki/Prime_minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime minister of India">6th Prime Minister of India</a> from 1984 to 1989. He took office after the <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Indira_Gandhi" title="Assassination of Indira Gandhi">assassination</a> of his mother, then–prime minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>, to become the youngest Indian prime minister at the age of 40. During his tenure, Gandhi introduced several initiatives and policies aimed at modernising India and promoting economic development. He emphasised technology, computerisation, and telecommunications, launching the "Vision 2020" program to transform India into a technologically advanced nation.
</p><p>Gandhi was not related to the world-famous <a href="/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi" title="Mahatma Gandhi">Mahatma Gandhi</a>. Instead, he was from the politically powerful <a href="/wiki/Nehru%E2%80%93Gandhi_family" title="Nehru–Gandhi family">Nehru–Gandhi family</a>, which had been associated with the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party. For much of his childhood, his maternal grandfather <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a> was prime minister. Gandhi attended the elite all-boys' boarding <a href="/wiki/The_Doon_School" title="The Doon School">The Doon School</a>, Dehradun, and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">University of Cambridge</a> in the United Kingdom. He returned to India in 1966 and became a professional pilot for the state-owned <a href="/wiki/Indian_Airlines" title="Indian Airlines">Indian Airlines</a>. In 1968, he married <a href="/wiki/Sonia_Maino" class="mw-redirect" title="Sonia Maino">Sonia Maino</a>; the couple settled in Delhi for a domestic life with their children <a href="/wiki/Rahul_Gandhi" title="Rahul Gandhi">Rahul</a> and <a href="/wiki/Priyanka_Gandhi" title="Priyanka Gandhi">Priyanka</a>. For much of the 1970s, his mother was prime minister and his brother <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi" title="Sanjay Gandhi">Sanjay</a> an MP; despite this, Gandhi remained apolitical. After Sanjay died in a plane crash in 1980, Gandhi reluctantly entered politics at the behest of his mother. The following year he won his brother's Parliamentary seat of <a href="/wiki/Amethi_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Amethi (Lok Sabha constituency)">Amethi</a> and became a member of the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a>—the lower house of <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_India" title="Parliament of India">India's Parliament</a>. As part of his political grooming, Rajiv was made general secretary of the Congress party and given significant responsibility in organising the <a href="/wiki/1982_Asian_Games" title="1982 Asian Games">1982 Asian Games</a>.
</p><p>On the morning of 31 October 1984, his mother (the then prime minister) was assassinated by her two <a href="/wiki/Sikh" class="mw-redirect" title="Sikh">Sikh</a> bodyguards<sup id="cite_ref-Ensaaf_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ensaaf-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bbconthisday_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbconthisday-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Satwant_Singh" title="Satwant Singh">Satwant Singh</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beant_Singh_(assassin)" title="Beant Singh (assassin)">Beant Singh</a> in the aftermath of <a href="/wiki/Operation_Blue_Star" title="Operation Blue Star">Operation Blue Star</a>, an Indian military action to remove <a href="/wiki/Dharam_Yudh_Morcha" title="Dharam Yudh Morcha">Sikh separatist activists</a> from the Golden temple of the <a href="/wiki/Harmandir_Sahib" class="mw-redirect" title="Harmandir Sahib">Harmandir Sahib</a>. Later that day, Gandhi was appointed prime minister. His leadership was tested over the next few days as organised mobs of Congress supporters <a href="/wiki/1984_anti-Sikh_riots" title="1984 anti-Sikh riots">rioted against the Sikh community</a>, resulting in anti-Sikh massacres in Delhi. Sources estimate the number of Sikh deaths at about 8,000–17,000.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> That December, the Congress party won the largest Lok Sabha majority to date, <a href="/wiki/1984_Indian_general_election" title="1984 Indian general election">411 seats out of 542</a>. Gandhi's period in office was mired in controversies; perhaps the greatest crises were the <a href="/wiki/Bhopal_disaster" title="Bhopal disaster">Bhopal disaster</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bofors_scandal" title="Bofors scandal">Bofors scandal</a> and <i><a href="/wiki/Mohd._Ahmed_Khan_v._Shah_Bano_Begum" title="Mohd. Ahmed Khan v. Shah Bano Begum">Mohd. Ahmed Khan v. Shah Bano Begum</a></i>. Soon after the installation of <a href="/wiki/Gul_Shah" class="mw-redirect" title="Gul Shah">Gul Shah</a> as chief minister in Jammu and Kashmir, the <a href="/wiki/1986_Kashmir_riots" title="1986 Kashmir riots">1986 Kashmir riots</a> erupted.<sup id="cite_ref-Aiyar2006_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Aiyar2006-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> In 1988, he reversed the <a href="/wiki/1988_Maldives_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat_attempt" class="mw-redirect" title="1988 Maldives coup d&#39;état attempt">coup in Maldives</a>, antagonising militant Tamil groups such as <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Liberation_Organisation_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="People&#39;s Liberation Organisation of Tamil Eelam">PLOTE</a>, intervening and then sending <a href="/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force" title="Indian Peace Keeping Force">peacekeeping</a> troops to Sri Lanka in 1987, leading to open conflict with the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam</a> (LTTE). In mid-1987, the Bofors scandal damaged his corruption-free image and resulted in a major defeat for his party in the <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 election</a>.
</p><p>Gandhi remained <a href="/wiki/Congress_president" class="mw-redirect" title="Congress president">Congress president</a> until the <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_general_election" title="1991 Indian general election">elections in 1991</a>. While campaigning for the elections, <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi">he was assassinated</a> by a suicide bomber from the LTTE. His widow Sonia became the president of the Congress party in 1998 and led the party to victory in the <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_general_election" title="2004 Indian general election">2004</a> and <a href="/wiki/2009_Indian_general_election" title="2009 Indian general election">2009</a> parliamentary elections. His son Rahul was a Member of Parliament until being disqualified in March 2023 after being convicted in a criminal defamation case and was the <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Indian_National_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="President of the Indian National Congress">President of the Indian National Congress</a> till 2019. In 1991, the Indian government posthumously awarded Gandhi the <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a>, the country's highest civilian award. At the India Leadership Conclave in 2009, the "Revolutionary Leader of Modern India" award was conferred posthumously on Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_career">Early life and career</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Indira_Gandhi,_Jawaharlal_Nehru,_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/220px-Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="277" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/330px-Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/440px-Indira_Gandhi%2C_Jawaharlal_Nehru%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1152" data-file-height="1450" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a>, Rajiv Gandhi and <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi" title="Sanjay Gandhi">Sanjay Gandhi</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajiv,_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/220px-Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/330px-Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg/440px-Rajiv%2C_Indira_and_Sanjay_Gandhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="607" data-file-height="390" /></a><figcaption>Rajiv Gandhi, <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi" title="Sanjay Gandhi">Sanjay Gandhi</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Rajiv Gandhi was born in <a href="/wiki/Bombay" class="mw-redirect" title="Bombay">Bombay</a> on 20 August 1944 to <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira</a> and <a href="/wiki/Feroze_Gandhi" title="Feroze Gandhi">Feroze Gandhi</a>. In 1951, Rajiv and his younger brother Sanjay were admitted to Shiv Niketan school, where the teachers said Rajiv was shy and introverted, and  "greatly enjoyed painting and drawing".<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He was admitted to the preparatory <a href="/wiki/Welham_Boys%27_School" title="Welham Boys&#39; School">Welham Boys' School</a> and then moved to <a href="/wiki/The_Doon_School" title="The Doon School">The Doon School</a>, Dehradun in 1954, where Sanjay joined him two years later.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> At Doon, Rajiv's senior was <a href="/wiki/Mani_Shankar_Aiyar" title="Mani Shankar Aiyar">Mani Shankar Aiyar</a>, who later became a prominent member in his inner circle.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Rajiv was also educated at the <a href="/wiki/Ecole_d%27Humanit%C3%A9" title="Ecole d&#39;Humanité">Ecole d'Humanité</a>, an international boarding school in Switzerland.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> He left the Doon School in 1961 with a second-class certificate, having performed well in his final subjects apart from a pass mark in chemistry.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During Gandhi's final year at Doon, his mother and Albert D'Rozario, the scientific <a href="/wiki/Attach%C3%A9" title="Attaché">attaché</a> at the <a href="/wiki/India_House,_London" title="India House, London">Indian High Commission in London</a>, arranged his application to <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">Cambridge University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> D'Rozario, who had been a college classmate of Feroze Gandhi, recommended that Gandhi should read engineering, and met with <a href="/wiki/M.G.M._Pryor" title="M.G.M. Pryor">Mark Pryor</a>, the Senior Tutor at <a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Trinity College, Cambridge</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Pryor arranged for Gandhi's conditional admission to Trinity, contingent on his passing the Mechanical Sciences Qualifying (MSQ) Examination with acceptable marks.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> After studying for his <a href="/wiki/A-Level" class="mw-redirect" title="A-Level">A-levels</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Sixth_form_college" title="Sixth form college">sixth form college</a> of <a href="/wiki/Abbey_DLD_Colleges_Group" title="Abbey DLD Colleges Group">Davies, Laing &amp; Dick</a> in London, Gandhi sat the MSQ Examination in March 1962 but was unsuccessful. He passed on his second attempt in June, and was admitted to Trinity on 4 September 1962, joining the college in October.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> While at Trinity, he joined the <a href="/wiki/Cambridge_University_Boat_Club" title="Cambridge University Boat Club">Cambridge University Boat Club</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During Gandhi's time at Cambridge, his mother and D'Rozario remained concerned about his well-being. D'Rozario, who along with his wife Sophy often hosted Gandhi at their <a href="/wiki/Finchley" title="Finchley">Finchley</a> home, took Gandhi to task for his inattention towards his studies.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Despite his support, Gandhi failed end-of-year exams and left Trinity in 1965 without a degree,<sup id="cite_ref-degree_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-degree-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> though he kept in touch with his former mentor in his retirement.<sup id="cite_ref-young_rajiv_15-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-young_rajiv-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> In 1966 he began a course in mechanical engineering at <a href="/wiki/Imperial_College_London" title="Imperial College London">Imperial College London</a>, but also failed to complete it. Gandhi really was not studious enough, as he went on to admit later.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gandhi returned to India in 1966, the year his mother became prime minister. He went to Delhi and became a member of the <a href="/wiki/Flying_Club" class="mw-redirect" title="Flying Club">Flying Club</a>, where he trained as a pilot. In 1970, he was employed as a pilot by <a href="/wiki/Indian_Airlines" title="Indian Airlines">Indian Airlines</a>; unlike Sanjay, he did not exhibit any interest of joining politics.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> In 1968, after three years of courtship, he married <a href="/wiki/Sonia_Gandhi" title="Sonia Gandhi">Edvige Antonia Albina Màino</a>, who changed her name to <a href="/wiki/Sonia_Gandhi" title="Sonia Gandhi">Sonia Gandhi</a> and made India her home. Their first child, a son, <a href="/wiki/Rahul_Gandhi" title="Rahul Gandhi">Rahul Gandhi</a> was born in 1970. In 1972, the couple had a daughter, <a href="/wiki/Priyanka_Gandhi" title="Priyanka Gandhi">Priyanka Gandhi</a>, who married <a href="/wiki/Robert_Vadra" title="Robert Vadra">Robert Vadra</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi was a friend of <a href="/wiki/Amitabh_Bachchan" title="Amitabh Bachchan">Amitabh Bachchan</a>, and was familiar with Bachchan even before he launched his acting career. Rajiv, Sanjay and Bachchan spent time together when Bachchan was student in <a href="/wiki/Delhi_University" title="Delhi University">Delhi University</a> and a resident of New Delhi. In the 1980s, Bachchan would enter politics to support his friend, Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Entry_into_politics">Entry into politics</span></h2>
<p>On 23 June 1980, Rajiv's younger brother <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi" title="Sanjay Gandhi">Sanjay Gandhi</a> died unexpectedly in an <a href="/wiki/Aeroplane" class="mw-redirect" title="Aeroplane">aeroplane</a> crash. At that time, Rajiv Gandhi was in London as part of his foreign tour. Hearing the news, he returned to Delhi and cremated Sanjay's body.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> As per Agarwal, in the week following Sanjay's death, Shankaracharya Swami Shri Swaroopanand, a saint from <a href="/wiki/Badrinath" title="Badrinath">Badrinath</a>, visited the family's house to offer his condolences.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> He advised Rajiv not to fly aeroplanes and instead "dedicate himself to the service of the nation".<sup id="cite_ref-Meenatt_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Meenatt-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> 70 members of the Congress party signed a proposal and went to Indira, urging Rajiv to enter politics. Indira told them it was Rajiv's decision whether to enter politics. When he was questioned about it, he replied, "If my mother gets help from it, then I will enter politics".<sup id="cite_ref-Meenatt_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Meenatt-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Rajiv entered politics on 16 February 1981, when he addressed a national farmers' rally in Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-TH_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TH-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> During this time, he was still an employee of Air India.<sup id="cite_ref-Anant_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anant-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Participation_in_active_politics">Participation in active politics</span></h3>
<p>On 4 May 1981, Indira Gandhi presided over a meeting of the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Congress_Committee" title="All India Congress Committee">All India Congress Committee</a>. <a href="/wiki/Vasantdada_Patil" title="Vasantdada Patil">Vasantdada Patil</a> proposed Rajiv as a candidate for the <a href="/wiki/Amethi_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Amethi (Lok Sabha constituency)">Amethi constituency</a>, which was accepted by all members at the meeting. A week later, the party officially announced his candidacy for the constituency. He then paid the party membership fees of the party and flew to <a href="/wiki/Sultanpur,_Uttar_Pradesh" title="Sultanpur, Uttar Pradesh">Sultanpur</a> to file his nomination papers and completed other formalities.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> He won the seat, defeating <a href="/wiki/Lok_Dal" class="mw-redirect" title="Lok Dal">Lok Dal</a> candidate <a href="/wiki/Sharad_Yadav" title="Sharad Yadav">Sharad Yadav</a> by a margin of 237,000 votes.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> He took his oath on 17 August as <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of Parliament">Member of Parliament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Anant_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anant-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rajiv Gandhi's first political tour was to England, where he attended the wedding ceremony of <a href="/wiki/Prince_Charles" class="mw-redirect" title="Prince Charles">Prince Charles</a> and <a href="/wiki/Diana,_Princess_of_Wales" title="Diana, Princess of Wales">Lady Diana Spencer</a> on 29 July 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-TF_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TF-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> In December the same year, he was put in charge of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Youth_Congress" title="Indian Youth Congress">Indian Youth Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-TF_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TF-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> He first showed his organisational ability by "working round the clock" on the <a href="/wiki/1982_Asian_Games" title="1982 Asian Games">1982 Asian Games</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He was one of 33 members of the Indian parliament who were part of the Games' organising committee; sports historian <a href="/wiki/Boria_Majumdar" title="Boria Majumdar">Boria Majumdar</a> writes that being "son of the prime minister he had a moral and unofficial authority" over the others.<sup id="cite_ref-Boria_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boria-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The report submitted by the Asian Games committee mentions Gandhi's "drive, zeal and initiative" for the "outstanding success" of the games.<sup id="cite_ref-Boria_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boria-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony,_1991.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg/220px-With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg/330px-With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg/440px-With_the_late_PM_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_during_the_medal_ceremony%2C_1991.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1392" data-file-height="912" /></a><figcaption>Rajiv Gandhi during a medal ceremony</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span id="1984_anti-Sikh_riots_post_Indira_Gandhi.27s_death"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="1984_anti-Sikh_riots_post_Indira_Gandhi's_death">1984 anti-Sikh riots post Indira Gandhi's death</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1984_anti-Sikh_riots" title="1984 anti-Sikh riots">1984 anti-Sikh riots</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg/220px-Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg/330px-Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg/440px-Rajiv_Gandhi_meeting_Russian_Hare_Krishna_devotees_in_New_Delhi_1989.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1713" data-file-height="1113" /></a><figcaption>Meeting Russian <a href="/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness" title="International Society for Krishna Consciousness">Hare Krishna</a> devotees in 1989.</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 31 October 1984, the prime minister, Rajiv Gandhi's mother, Indira Gandhi, was assassinated by her Sikh bodyguards, which led to violent riots against Sikhs.<sup id="cite_ref-AS_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AS-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> At a Boat Club rally 19 days after the assassination, Gandhi said, "Some riots took place in the country following the murder of Indiraji. We know the people were very angry and for a few days it seemed that India had been shaken. But, when a mighty tree falls, it is only natural that the earth around it does shake a little".<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> According to Verinder Grover, the statement made by Gandhi was a "virtual justification" of the riots.<sup id="cite_ref-AS_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AS-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Congress leader <a href="/wiki/Mani_Shankar_Aiyar" title="Mani Shankar Aiyar">Mani Shankar Aiyar</a> wrote, "Did it constitute an incitement to mass murder?" He also criticised Gandhi for his reluctance to bring the army from <a href="/wiki/Meerut" title="Meerut">Meerut</a> to handle the mob.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_of_India">Prime Minister of India</span></h2>
<p>Rajiv Gandhi was in West Bengal on 31 October 1984 when his mother, Prime Minister Indira Gandhi, was assassinated by two of her Sikh bodyguards, <a href="/wiki/Satwant_Singh" title="Satwant Singh">Satwant Singh</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beant_Singh_(assassin)" title="Beant Singh (assassin)">Beant Singh</a>, to avenge the military attack on the <a href="/wiki/Harmandir_Sahib" class="mw-redirect" title="Harmandir Sahib">Golden Temple</a> during <a href="/wiki/Operation_Blue_Star" title="Operation Blue Star">Operation Blue Star</a>. Sardar Buta Singh and President <a href="/wiki/Zail_Singh" title="Zail Singh">Zail Singh</a> pressed Rajiv to succeed his mother as prime minister within hours of her murder. Commenting on the anti-Sikh riots in Delhi, Rajiv Gandhi said, "When a giant tree falls, the earth below shakes";<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> a statement for which he was widely criticised. Many Congress politicians were accused of orchestrating the violence.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1062260506">.mw-parser-output .quotebox{background-color:#F9F9F9;border:1px solid #aaa;box-sizing:border-box;padding:10px;font-size:88%;max-width:100%}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft{margin:.5em 1.4em .8em 0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright{margin:.5em 0 .8em 1.4em}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.centered{overflow:hidden;position:relative;margin:.5em auto .8em auto}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft span,.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright span{font-style:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox>blockquote{margin:0;padding:0;border-left:0;font-family:inherit;font-size:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-title{background-color:#F9F9F9;text-align:center;font-size:110%;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote>:first-child{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote:last-child>:last-child{margin-bottom:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:before{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" “ ";vertical-align:-45%;line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:after{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" ” ";line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .left-aligned{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .right-aligned{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .center-aligned{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quote-title,.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quotebox-quote{display:block}.mw-parser-output .quotebox cite{display:block;font-style:normal}@media screen and (max-width:640px){.mw-parser-output .quotebox{width:100%!important;margin:0 0 .8em!important;float:none!important}}</style><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:20em; ;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>Indian politics got the youngest ever Prime minister in Rajiv Gandhi. This phenomenon attracted attention the world over. . . his winsome smile, charm and decency were his valuable personal assets. . . A senior opposition member, while talking to me, conceded that . . . he could not conceal his feeling that Rajiv Gandhi would be invincible for the opposition.
</p>
</blockquote>
<p><cite class="left-aligned" style="">— <i><a href="/wiki/Satyendra_Narayan_Sinha" title="Satyendra Narayan Sinha">Satyendra Narayan Sinha</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup></cite>
</p>
</div>
<p>Soon after assuming office, Gandhi asked President Singh to dissolve Parliament and hold fresh elections, as the Lok Sabha had completed its five-year term. Gandhi officially became the president of the Congress party, which won a landslide victory with the largest majority in history of the Indian Parliament, giving Gandhi absolute control of government. He benefited from his youth and a general perception of being free of a background in corrupt politics.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi took his oath on 31 December 1984; at 40,  he was the youngest prime minister of India.<sup id="cite_ref-oath_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oath-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Historian Meena Agarwal writes that even after taking the Prime Ministerial oath, he was a relatively unknown figure, "novice in politics" as he assumed the post after being an MP for three years.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_roles">Prime Minister roles</span></h3>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cabinet_ministers">Cabinet ministers</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajiv Gandhi ministry">Rajiv Gandhi ministry</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg/220px-Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="363" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg/330px-Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg/440px-Rajiv_Gandhi_at_The_Doon_School_Founder%27s_Day.jpg 2x" data-file-width="537" data-file-height="886" /></a><figcaption>Gandhi's inner circle was labelled 'Doon Cabinet' or 'Dosco Mafia', given the induction of many of his <a href="/wiki/Doon_School" class="mw-redirect" title="Doon School">Doon School</a> acquaintances (<i>Gandhi pictured in the Doon School blazer during a Founder's Day visit</i>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>After his swearing-in as prime minister, Gandhi appointed his fourteen-member cabinet. He said he would monitor their performance and would "fire ministers who do not come to the mark".<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> From the <a href="/wiki/Third_Indira_Gandhi_ministry" title="Third Indira Gandhi ministry">Third Indira Gandhi ministry</a>, he removed two powerful figures; Finance Minister <a href="/wiki/Pranab_Mukherjee" title="Pranab Mukherjee">Pranab Mukherjee</a> and Railway Minister <a href="/wiki/A._B._A._Ghani_Khan_Choudhury" title="A. B. A. Ghani Khan Choudhury">A. B. A. Ghani Khan Choudhury</a>. <a href="/wiki/Mohsina_Kidwai" title="Mohsina Kidwai">Mohsina Kidwai</a> became the Minister of Railways; she was the only female figure in the cabinet. Former Home Minister <a href="/wiki/PV_Narasimha_Rao" class="mw-redirect" title="PV Narasimha Rao">PV Narasimha Rao</a> was put in charge of <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(India)" title="Minister of Defence (India)">defence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-oath_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oath-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a>, who was initially appointed as the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">Finance Minister</a>, was given the Defence Ministry in 1987.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> During his tenure as prime minister, Gandhi frequently shuffled his cabinet ministers, drawing criticism from the magazine <i><a href="/wiki/India_Today" title="India Today">India Today</a></i>, which called it a "wheel of confusion". The <a href="/wiki/West_Bengal" title="West Bengal">West Bengal</a> chief minister <a href="/wiki/Jyoti_Basu" title="Jyoti Basu">Jyoti Basu</a> said, "The Cabinet change reflects the instability of the Congress (I) Government at the Centre".<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> He also administered and created the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Environment,_Forest_and_Climate_Change" title="Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change">Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change</a>.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dosco_Mafia_or_Doon_Cabinet">Dosco Mafia or Doon Cabinet</span></h4>
<p>Gandhi, an alumnus of <a href="/wiki/The_Doon_School" title="The Doon School">The Doon School</a>, drew criticism from the media for appointing many old boys to his administration.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> His inner circle was labelled a "Doon Cabinet"<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> or "Dosco Mafia",<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> and <i>Washington Post</i> reported, "The catch phrase around Delhi these days is that the 'Doon School runs India,' but that is too simple an analysis for a complex, chaotic country with so many competing spheres of influence."<sup id="cite_ref-bumiller1985_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bumiller1985-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi's reliance on Doon alumni for political advice later led Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a> to remark, "If I had anything to do with this place, I'd close it down".<sup id="cite_ref-bumiller1985_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bumiller1985-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Anti-defection_law">Anti-defection law</span></h3>
<p>Gandhi's first action as prime minister was passing the <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">anti-defection law</a> in January 1985. According to this law, an elected Member of Parliament or legislative assembly could not join an opposition party until the next election. Historian Manish Telikicherla Chary calls it a measure of curbing corruption and bribery of ministers by switching parties so they could gain majority.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Many such defections occurred during the 1980s as elected leaders of the Congress party joined opposition parties.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="1985_Congress_Sandesh_Yatra">1985 Congress Sandesh Yatra</span></h3>
<p>Rajiv Gandhi had announced 'Sandesh Yatra' at the plenary session of AICC in Mumbai in 1985. The All India Congress Seva Dal ran it across the country.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Pradesh Congress Committees (PCCs) and party leaders made four simultaneous trips from Mumbai, Kashmir, Kanyakumari and the Northeast. The yatra, which lasted for more than three months, concluded at Delhi's Ramlila Maidan.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mass_connect_programmes_though_Bharat_Yatra">Mass connect programmes though Bharat Yatra</span></h3>
<p>In 1990, Rajiv Gandhi undertook Bharat Yatra through different modes – padyatra, the second class carriage of an ordinary passenger train.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> He chose <a href="/wiki/Champaran" title="Champaran">Champaran</a> as the starting point for his 'Bharat Yatra'. Rajiv Gandhi  started the <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_National_Sadbhavana_Award" title="Rajiv Gandhi National Sadbhavana Award">Sadbhavna Yatra</a> from <a href="/wiki/Charminar" title="Charminar">Charminar</a> in Hyderabad on 19 October 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Shah_Bano_case">Shah Bano case</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Shah_Bano_case" class="mw-redirect" title="Shah Bano case">Shah Bano case</a></div>
<p>In 1985, the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_India" title="Supreme Court of India">Supreme Court of India</a> ruled in favour of Muslim divorcee <a href="/wiki/Shah_Bano" class="mw-redirect" title="Shah Bano">Shah Bano</a>, declaring that her husband should give her <a href="/wiki/Alimony" title="Alimony">alimony</a>. Some Indian Muslims treated it as an encroachment upon <a href="/wiki/Muslim_Personal_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim Personal Law">Muslim Personal Law</a> and protested against it. Gandhi agreed to their demands.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> In 1986, the Parliament of India passed <a href="/wiki/The_Muslim_Women_(Protection_of_Rights_on_Divorce)_Act_1986" class="mw-redirect" title="The Muslim Women (Protection of Rights on Divorce) Act 1986">The Muslim Women (Protection of Rights on Divorce) Act 1986</a>, which nullified the Supreme Court's judgment in the Shah Bano case. The Act diluted the Supreme Court judgment and allowed maintenance payments to divorced women only during the period of <a href="/wiki/Iddah" title="Iddah">Iddah</a>, or until 90 days after the divorce, according to the provisions of Islamic law. This was in contrast to Section 125 of the Code.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> Indian magazine <i>Business and Economics</i> called it a minority appeasement by Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Lawyer and former Law Minister of India, <a href="/wiki/Ram_Jethmalani" title="Ram Jethmalani">Ram Jethmalani</a>, called the Act "retrogressive obscurantism for short-term minority populism".<sup id="cite_ref-medio_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-medio-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi's colleague <a href="/wiki/Arif_Mohammad_Khan" title="Arif Mohammad Khan">Arif Mohammad Khan</a>, who was then a Member of Parliament, resigned in protest.<sup id="cite_ref-indiaafterind_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indiaafterind-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_policy">Economic policy</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7,_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg/220px-Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg/330px-Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg/440px-Pandit_Ram_Kishore_Shukla_with_Rajiv_Gandhi_at_7%2C_Race_course_road_in_1988.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4200" data-file-height="3000" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Rajiv Gandhi with <a href="/wiki/Ram_Kishore_Shukla" title="Ram Kishore Shukla">Ram Kishore Shukla</a> in 1988.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In his election manifesto for the <a href="/wiki/1984_Indian_general_election" title="1984 Indian general election">1984 general election</a>, he did not mention any economic reforms, but after assuming office he tried to liberalise the country's economy.<sup id="cite_ref-EP_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EP-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> He sought to liberalise India's trade policies but faced stiff opposition to the proposed reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EP_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EP-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> He did so by providing incentives to make private production profitable. Subsidies were given to corporate companies to increase industrial production, especially of <a href="/wiki/Durable_goods" class="mw-redirect" title="Durable goods">durable goods</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a class="mw-selflink-fragment" href="#Economic_policy"><span title="Subsidies to firms is not a liberal market reform (October 2022)">contradictory</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> It was hoped this would increase economic growth and improve the quality of investment.<sup id="cite_ref-EP_59-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EP-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Rural and tribal people protested because they saw them as "pro-rich" and "pro-city" reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-EP_59-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EP-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gandhi increased government support for science, technology and associated industries, and reduced import quotas, taxes and tariffs on technology-based industries, especially computers, airlines, defence and telecommunications. In 1986, he announced a <a href="/wiki/National_Policy_on_Education" title="National Policy on Education">National Policy on Education</a> to modernise and expand higher education programs across India. In 1986, he founded the <a href="/wiki/Jawahar_Navodaya_Vidyalaya" title="Jawahar Navodaya Vidyalaya">Jawahar Navodaya Vidyalaya</a> System, which is a Central government-based education institution that provides rural populations with free residential education from grades six to twelve.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> His efforts created <a href="/wiki/MTNL" class="mw-redirect" title="MTNL">MTNL</a> in 1986, and his public call offices—better known as PCOs—helped develop the telephone network in rural areas.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> He introduced measures to significantly reduce the <i><a href="/wiki/Licence_Raj" title="Licence Raj">Licence Raj</a></i> after 1990, allowing businesses and individuals to purchase capital, consumer goods and import without bureaucratic restrictions.<sup id="cite_ref-princeton.edu_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-princeton.edu-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy">Foreign policy</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Ronald_Reagan,_Nancy_Reagan,_Rajiv_Gandhi,_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg/220px-President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg/330px-President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg/440px-President_Ronald_Reagan%2C_Nancy_Reagan%2C_Rajiv_Gandhi%2C_and_Sonia_Gandhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="2653" /></a><figcaption>President Ronald Reagan, Sonia Gandhi, First Lady Nancy Reagan and Prime Minister Rajiv Gandhi, during a state dinner for Prime Minister Gandhi. June 1985.</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Rejaul_Karim_Laskar" title="Rejaul Karim Laskar">Rejaul Karim Laskar</a>, a scholar of <a href="/wiki/Indian_foreign_policy" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian foreign policy">Indian foreign policy</a> and an ideologue of <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress party</a>, Rajiv Gandhi's vision for a new world order was premised on <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>'s place in its front rank.<sup id="cite_ref-diplomatist.com_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-diplomatist.com-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Rejaul_Karim_Laskar" title="Rejaul Karim Laskar">Laskar</a>, the "whole gamut" of Rajiv Gandhi's <a href="/wiki/Foreign_policy" title="Foreign policy">foreign policy</a> was "geared towards" making India "strong, independent, self-reliant and in the front rank of the nations of the world."<sup id="cite_ref-diplomatist.com_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-diplomatist.com-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Rejaul_Karim_Laskar" title="Rejaul Karim Laskar">Laskar</a>, Rajiv Gandhi's diplomacy was "properly calibrated" so as to be "conciliatory and accommodating when required" and "assertive when the occasion demanded."<sup id="cite_ref-diplomatist.com_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-diplomatist.com-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1986, by request of the president of Seychelles <a href="/wiki/France-Albert_Ren%C3%A9" title="France-Albert René">France-Albert René</a>, Gandhi sent India's navy to <a href="/wiki/Seychelles" title="Seychelles">Seychelles</a> to oppose an attempted coup against René. The intervention of India averted the coup. This mission was codenamed as <a href="/wiki/Operation_Flowers_are_Blooming" title="Operation Flowers are Blooming">Operation Flowers are Blooming</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> In 1987, India re-occupied the Quaid Post in the disputed <a href="/wiki/Siachen" class="mw-redirect" title="Siachen">Siachen</a> region of the <a href="/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Indo-Pakistani border">Indo-Pakistani border</a> after winning what was termed <a href="/wiki/Operation_Rajiv" title="Operation Rajiv">Operation Rajiv</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/1988_Maldives_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" class="mw-redirect" title="1988 Maldives coup d&#39;état">1988 Maldives coup d'état</a>, the Maldives president <a href="/wiki/Maumoon_Abdul_Gayoom" title="Maumoon Abdul Gayoom">Maumoon Abdul Gayoom</a> asked for help from Gandhi. He dispatched 1500 soldiers and the coup was suppressed.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On Thursday, 9 June 1988, at the fifteenth special session of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a>, held at Headquarters, New York, Gandhi made vocal his views on a world free of nuclear weapons, to be realised through an, 'Action Plan for Ushering in a Nuclear-Weapon Free and Non-Violent World Order.'<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He said:
</p>
<blockquote><p>Alas, nuclear weapons are not the only weapons of mass destruction. New knowledge is being generated in the life sciences. Military applications of these developments could rapidly undermine the existing convention against the military use of biological weapons. The ambit of our concern must extend to all means of mass <a href="/wiki/Annihilation" title="Annihilation">annihilation</a>.</p></blockquote>
<p>This was based on his prior historic speech before the Japanese <a href="/wiki/National_Diet" title="National Diet">National Diet</a> on 29 November 1985, in which he said:
</p>
<blockquote><p>Let us remove the mental partitions which obstruct the ennobling vision of the human family linked together in peace and prosperity. The Buddha's message of compassion is the very condition of human survival in our age.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The foiled bid of India recently to enter the <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_Suppliers_Group" title="Nuclear Suppliers Group">Nuclear Suppliers Group</a>, echoed his policy of non-proliferation to be linked to universal disarmament, which the <a href="/wiki/World_Nuclear_Association" title="World Nuclear Association">World Nuclear Association</a> refuses to recognise; non-proliferation being seen by India as essentially a weapon of the arms control regime, of the big nuclear powers as United States, Russia, United Kingdom, France, and China.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Pakistan">Pakistan</span></h4>
<p>In February 1987, the Pakistani president <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Zia-ul-Haq" title="Muhammad Zia-ul-Haq">Zia-ul-Haq</a> visited Delhi, where he met Gandhi to discuss "routine military exercises of the Indian army" on the borders of <a href="/wiki/Rajasthan" title="Rajasthan">Rajasthan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_India" title="Punjab, India">Punjab</a>. Gandhi reciprocated, in December 1988, by visiting <a href="/wiki/Islamabad" title="Islamabad">Islamabad</a> and meeting the new prime minister of Pakistan, <a href="/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto" title="Benazir Bhutto">Benazir Bhutto</a>, to reaffirm the <a href="/wiki/1972_Shimla_agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="1972 Shimla agreement">1972 Shimla agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sharma,_p._16_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sharma,_p._16-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Sri_Lanka">Sri Lanka</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_civil_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Sri Lankan civil war">Sri Lankan civil war</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indian_intervention_in_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Indian intervention in the Sri Lankan Civil War">Indian intervention in the Sri Lankan Civil War</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Sri Lankan Civil War">Sri Lankan Civil War</a> broke out with the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam</a> (LTTE), which was demanding an independent <a href="/wiki/Tamil_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Tamil people">Tamil</a> state in Sri Lanka. Gandhi discussed the matter with the Sri Lankan Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ranasinghe_Premadasa" title="Ranasinghe Premadasa">Ranasinghe Premadasa</a> at the <a href="/wiki/SAARC" class="mw-redirect" title="SAARC">SAARC</a> meeting in 1986. In that year, the Sri Lankan army blockaded the Tamil majority district of <a href="/wiki/Jaffna_District" title="Jaffna District">Jaffna</a>; Gandhi ordered relief supplies to be dropped into the area by parachute because the Sri Lankan navy did not allow the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Navy" title="Indian Navy">Indian Navy</a> to enter.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gandhi signed the <a href="/wiki/Indo-Sri_Lanka_Accord" title="Indo-Sri Lanka Accord">Indo-Sri Lanka Accord</a> in July 1987. The accord "envisaged a devolution of power to the Tamil-majority areas", dissolved the LTTE, and designated <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a> as an official language of Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-SL_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SL-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi said:
</p>
<blockquote><p>The Government of India believe that, despite some problems and delays, many of which were foreseen but unavoidable in the resolution of an issue of this magnitude and complexity, this Agreement represents the only way of safeguarding legitimate Tamil interests and ensuring a durable peace in Sri Lanka. Some have chosen to criticise the Agreement. None has shown a better way of meeting the legitimate aspirations of the Tamils in Sri Lanka, restoring peace in that country and of meeting our own security concern in the region. We have accepted a role which is difficult, but which is in our national interests to discharge. We shall not shrink our obligations and commitments. This is a national endeavour.<sup id="cite_ref-SL_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SL-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Chanderasekar withdrew the IPKF in 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-Sharma,_p._16_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sharma,_p._16-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Assault_by_Sri_Lankan_guard">Assault by Sri Lankan guard</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_assassination_attempts_on_prime_ministers_of_India" title="List of assassination attempts on prime ministers of India">List of assassination attempts on prime ministers of India</a></div>
<p>On 30 July 1987, a day after Gandhi went to Sri Lanka and signed the Indo-Sri Lanka Accord, an honour guard named <a href="/wiki/Vijitha_Rohana" title="Vijitha Rohana">Vijitha Rohana</a> hit him on his shoulder with his rifle; Gandhi's quick reflexes saved him from injury. The guard was then dragged off by his security personnel.<sup id="cite_ref-NIE_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NIE-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-injure_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-injure-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The guard said his intention was to kill Gandhi because of "the damage he had caused" to Sri Lanka. Wijemuni was imprisoned for two-and-a-half years for the assault.<sup id="cite_ref-NIE_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NIE-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi later said about the incident:
</p>
<blockquote><p>When I was inspecting the guard of honour and as I walked past one person, I saw through the corner of my eye some movement. I ducked down a little bit in a reflex action. By my ducking, he missed my head and the brunt of the blow came on my shoulder below the left ear.<sup id="cite_ref-injure_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-injure-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Regional_issues">Regional issues</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg/220px-Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg/330px-Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg/440px-Bajaj-Polar-3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1279" data-file-height="865" /></a><figcaption>Rajiv Gandhi (left) congratulates Indian Army explorers for reaching the South Pole.</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Punjab">Punjab</span></h4>
<p>Soon after assuming office, Gandhi released the leaders of the <a href="/wiki/Akali_Dal" class="mw-redirect" title="Akali Dal">Akali Dal</a> who had been imprisoned since 1984's Operation Blue Star during Indira Gandhi's prime ministership. He lifted the ban on <a href="/wiki/All_India_Sikh_Students_Federation" title="All India Sikh Students Federation">All India Sikh Students Federation</a> and filed an inquiry into the <a href="/wiki/1984_Anti-Sikh_Riots" class="mw-redirect" title="1984 Anti-Sikh Riots">1984 Anti-Sikh Riots</a>. He also held a closed-door meeting with senior Akali Dal leaders to find a solution to the Punjab problem. Despite Akali opposition, in January 1985, Gandhi signed the <a href="/wiki/Rajiv-Longowal_Accord" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajiv-Longowal Accord">Rajiv-Longowal Accord</a> with Akali leader HS Longowal. Punjab's state assembly election was scheduled in September 1985, but Longowal died and was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Surjit_Singh_Barnala" title="Surjit Singh Barnala">Surjit Singh Barnala</a>, who formed the government. After two years, in 1987, Barnala resigned his office because of a breakdown of law and order, leading to the implementation of <a href="/wiki/President%27s_rule" title="President&#39;s rule">President's rule</a> in the state.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 1988, Gandhi launched the <a href="/wiki/Operation_Black_Thunder" title="Operation Black Thunder">Operation Black Thunder</a> to clear the Golden Temple in <a href="/wiki/Amritsar" title="Amritsar">Amritsar</a> of arms and gunmen. Two groups called National Security Guard and Special Action Group were created; they surrounded the temple in a 10-day siege during which the extremists' weapons were confiscated. Congress leader <a href="/wiki/Anand_Sharma" title="Anand Sharma">Anand Sharma</a> said, "Operation Black Thunder effectively demonstrated the will of Rajiv Gandhi's government to take firm action to bring peace to Punjab".<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Northeast_India">Northeast India</span></h4>
<p>Gandhi's prime-ministership marked an increase of <a href="/wiki/Insurgency_in_Northeast_India" title="Insurgency in Northeast India">insurgency in northeast India</a>. <a href="/wiki/Mizo_National_Front" title="Mizo National Front">Mizo National Front</a> demanded independence for <a href="/wiki/Mizoram" title="Mizoram">Mizoram</a>. In 1987, Gandhi addressed this problem; Mizoram and <a href="/wiki/Arunachal_Pradesh" title="Arunachal Pradesh">Arunachal Pradesh</a> were given the status of states that were earlier union territories.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi also ended the <a href="/wiki/Assam_Movement" title="Assam Movement">Assam Movement</a>, which was launched by Assamese people to protest against the alleged illegal migration of Bangladeshi Muslims and immigration of other Bengalis to their state, which had reduced the Assamese to a minority there. He signed the <a href="/wiki/Assam_Accord" title="Assam Accord">Assam Accord</a> on 15 August 1985. According to the accord, foreigners who came to the state between 1951 and 1961 were given full citizenship but those who arrived there between 1961 and 1971 did not get right to vote for the next ten years.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Technology">Technology</span></h3>
<p>Gandhi employed former <a href="/wiki/Rockwell_International" title="Rockwell International">Rockwell International</a> executive <a href="/wiki/Sam_Pitroda" title="Sam Pitroda">Sam Pitroda</a> as his adviser on public information infrastructure and innovation. During Gandhi's time in office, public sector telecom companies <a href="/wiki/MTNL" class="mw-redirect" title="MTNL">MTNL</a> and <a href="/wiki/VSNL" class="mw-redirect" title="VSNL">VSNL</a> was developed.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> According to Pitroda, Gandhi's ability to resist pressure from multi-national companies to abandon his plan to spread telecommunication services has been an important factor in India's development. According to news website <a href="/wiki/Oneindia" title="Oneindia">Oneindia</a>, "About 20 years ago telephones were considered to be a thing for the use of the rich, but credit goes to Rajiv Gandhi for taking them to the rural masses".<sup id="cite_ref-RG_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RG-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Pitroda also said their plan to expand India's telephone network succeeded because of Gandhi's political support. According to Pitroda, by 2007 they were "adding six million phones every month".<sup id="cite_ref-RG_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RG-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi's government also allowed the import of fully assembled motherboards, which led to the price of computers being reduced.<sup id="cite_ref-tel_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tel-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> According to some commentators,  the seed for the information technology (IT) revolution was also planted during Rajiv Gandhi's time.<sup id="cite_ref-tel_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tel-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Bofors_scandal.2C_HDW_scandal_and_1989_elections_defeat"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Bofors_scandal,_HDW_scandal_and_1989_elections_defeat">Bofors scandal, HDW scandal and 1989 elections defeat</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Bofors_scandal" title="Bofors scandal">Bofors scandal</a></div>
<p>Rajiv Gandhi's finance minister, <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a>, uncovered compromising details about government and political corruption, to the consternation of Congress leaders. Transferred to the Defence Ministry, Singh uncovered what became known as the Bofors scandal, which involved millions of US dollars and concerned alleged payoffs by the Swedish arms company <a href="/wiki/Bofors" title="Bofors">Bofors</a> through Italian businessman and Gandhi family associate <a href="/wiki/Ottavio_Quattrocchi" title="Ottavio Quattrocchi">Ottavio Quattrocchi</a>, in return for Indian contracts. Upon discovering the scandal, Singh was dismissed from office and later resigned his Congress membership. Gandhi was later personally implicated in the scandal when the investigation was continued by <a href="/wiki/Narasimhan_Ram" class="mw-redirect" title="Narasimhan Ram">Narasimhan Ram</a> and Chitra Subramaniam of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Hindu" title="The Hindu">The Hindu</a></i> newspaper, damaging his image as an honest politician. In 2004, he was posthumously cleared of this allegation.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In an interview in July 2005, V. P. Singh explained that his fall out with Rajiv Gandhi was not due to the Bofors deal, but rather due to the <a href="/wiki/Shishumar-class_submarine" title="Shishumar-class submarine">HDW deal</a>. Courtesy a contract signed with the German 
company <a href="/wiki/HDW" class="mw-redirect" title="HDW">HDW</a> in 1981, the Indian government had agreed to purchase two ready submarines built in Germany by HDW and two submarines in CKD form to be assembled in Mazagaon docks. V. P. Singh had received a telegram from the Indian ambassador in Germany, stating that an Indian agents had received commissions in the <a href="/wiki/Shishumar-class_submarine" title="Shishumar-class submarine">HDW submarine</a> deal. He told Rajiv Gandhi about this and instituted an enquiry. This led to differences and V. P. Singh resigned from the cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-vp_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vp-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his book, <i>Unknown Facets of Rajiv Gandhi, Jyoti Basu and Indrajit Gupta</i>, released in November 2013, former <a href="/wiki/Central_Bureau_of_Investigation" title="Central Bureau of Investigation">CBI</a> director Dr. A P Mukherjee wrote that Gandhi wanted commission paid by defence suppliers to be used exclusively for meeting running expenses of the Congress party.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Mukherjee said Gandhi explained his position in a meeting between the two at the prime minister's residence on 19 June 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In May 2015, Indian president Pranab Mukherjee said the scandal was a "media trial" as "no Indian court has as yet established it as a scandal".<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Opposition parties Lok Dal, <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Socialist)" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian National Congress (Socialist)">Indian National Congress (Socialist)</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a> united under Singh to form the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Singh led the <a href="/wiki/National_Front_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Front (India)">National Front</a> coalition to victory in <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 elections</a> and he was sworn in as prime minister. Though the coalition won 143 seats compared to Congress's 197, it gained majority in the lower house of the parliament through outside support from the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janta_Party" class="mw-redirect" title="Bharatiya Janta Party">Bharatiya Janta Party</a> under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lal_Krishna_Advani" class="mw-redirect" title="Lal Krishna Advani">Lal Krishna Advani</a> and the left parties such as the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India_(Marxist)" title="Communist Party of India (Marxist)">Communist Party of India (Marxist)</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India" title="Communist Party of India">Communist Party of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Eminent lawyer and politician, former <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Law_and_Justice_(India)" title="Ministry of Law and Justice (India)">Law Minister of India</a> <a href="/wiki/Ram_Jethmalani" title="Ram Jethmalani">Ram Jethmalani</a> said that as prime minister, Gandhi was "lacklustre and mediocre".<sup id="cite_ref-medio_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-medio-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Posthumous_reports">Posthumous reports</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Allegations_of_black_money">Allegations of black money</span></h3>
<p>In November 1991, <i><a href="/wiki/Schweizer_Illustrierte" title="Schweizer Illustrierte">Schweizer Illustrierte</a></i> magazine published an article on <a href="/wiki/Black_money" class="mw-redirect" title="Black money">black money</a> held in secret accounts by <a href="/wiki/Imelda_Marcos" title="Imelda Marcos">Imelda Marcos</a> and 14 other rulers of <a href="/wiki/Third_World" title="Third World">Third World</a> countries. Citing <a href="/wiki/McKinsey_%26_Company" title="McKinsey &amp; Company">McKinsey</a> as a source, the article stated that Rajiv Gandhi held 2.5 billion <a href="/wiki/Swiss_franc" title="Swiss franc">Swiss francs</a> in secret Indian accounts in Switzerland.<sup id="cite_ref-jethmalani_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jethmalani-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Gisler_38–41_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gisler_38–41-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Several leaders of opposition parties in India raised the issue, citing the <i>Schweizer Illustrierte</i> article. In December 1991, Amal Datta raised the issue in the Indian Parliament; the Speaker of the Lok Sabha, <a href="/wiki/Shivraj_Patil" title="Shivraj Patil">Shivraj Patil</a>, expunged Rajiv Gandhi's name from the proceedings.<sup id="cite_ref-gurumurthy_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gurumurthy-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> In December 2011, <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a> wrote to the director of the Central Bureau of Investigation, citing the article and asking him to take action on black money accounts of the <a href="/wiki/Nehru%E2%80%93Gandhi_family" title="Nehru–Gandhi family">Nehru-Gandhi family</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> On 29 December 2011, Ram Jethmalani made an indirect reference to the issue in the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>, calling it a shame that one of India's former prime ministers was named by a Swiss magazine. This was met by an uproar and a demand for withdrawal of the remark by the ruling Congress party members.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Funding_from_Russian_KGB_secret_police">Funding from Russian KGB secret police</span></h3>
<p>In 1992, the Indian newspapers <i><a href="/wiki/Times_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Times of India">Times of India</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/The_Hindu" title="The Hindu">The Hindu</a></i> published reports alleging that Rajiv Gandhi had received funds from the <a href="/wiki/KGB" title="KGB">KGB</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-gurumurthy_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gurumurthy-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The Russian government confirmed this disclosure and defended the payments as necessary for Soviet ideological interest.<sup id="cite_ref-puri_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-puri-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In their 1994 book <i>The State Within a State</i>, journalists <a href="/wiki/Yevgenia_Albats" title="Yevgenia Albats">Yevgenia Albats</a> and <a href="/wiki/Catherine_A._Fitzpatrick" title="Catherine A. Fitzpatrick">Catherine Fitzpatrick</a> quoted a letter signed by <a href="/wiki/Viktor_Chebrikov" title="Viktor Chebrikov">Viktor Chebrikov</a>, head of the KGB, in the 1980s. The letter says the KGB maintained contact with Gandhi, who expressed his gratitude to the KGB for benefits accruing to his family from commercial dealings of a controlled firm. A considerable portion of funds obtained from this channel were used to support his party.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> Albats later said that in December 1985, Chebrikov had asked for authorisation from the <a href="/wiki/Central_Committee_of_the_Communist_Party_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Central Committee of the Communist Party of the Soviet Union">Central Committee of the Communist Party of the Soviet Union</a> to make payments to family members of Rajiv Gandhi, including Sonia Gandhi and Rahul Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-gurumurthy_96-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gurumurthy-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-puri_99-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-puri-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The payments were authorised by a resolution and endorsed by the USSR Council of Ministers, and had been paid since 1971.<sup id="cite_ref-puri_99-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-puri-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In December 2001, Subramanian Swamy filed a writ petition in the Delhi High Court; the court ordered CBI to ascertain the truth of the allegations in May 2002. After two years, the CBI told the Court Russia would not entertain such queries without a registered FIR.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (November 2018)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-puri_99-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-puri-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Assassination">Assassination</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi">Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi</a></div><p>
Rajiv Gandhi's last public meeting was on 21 May 1991, at <a href="/wiki/Sriperumbudur" title="Sriperumbudur">Sriperumbudur</a>, a village approximately 40&#160;km (25&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Madras" class="mw-redirect" title="Madras">Madras</a> (present-day Chennai), where he was assassinated while campaigning for the Sriperumbudur Lok Sabha Congress candidate. At 10:10 pm, a woman later identified as <a href="/wiki/Thenmozhi_Rajaratnam" class="mw-redirect" title="Thenmozhi Rajaratnam">Thenmozhi Rajaratnam</a> – a member of the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam</a> – approached Gandhi in public and greeted him. She then bent down to touch his feet and detonated a belt laden with 700&#160;g (1.5&#160;lb) of <a href="/wiki/RDX" title="RDX">RDX</a> explosives tucked under her dress.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> </p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg/220px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg/330px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg/440px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial-Delhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat" class="mw-redirect" title="Raj Ghat">Veer Bhumi</a></i> at Delhi, where Rajiv&#160;Gandhi was cremated</figcaption></figure><p>The explosion killed Gandhi, Rajaratnam, and at least 14 other people.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The assassination was captured by a 21-year-old local photographer, whose camera and film were found at the site. The cameraman, named Haribabu, died in the blast but the camera remained intact.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi's mutilated body was airlifted to the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Institute_of_Medical_Sciences" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Institute of Medical Sciences">All India Institute of Medical Sciences</a> in New Delhi for post-mortem, reconstruction and embalming.<sup id="cite_ref-rajivgandhifuneral_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rajivgandhifuneral-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
