</p><p>A state funeral was held for Gandhi on 24 May 1991; it was telecast live and was attended by dignitaries from over 60 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> He was cremated at <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Vir Bhumi</a>, on the banks of the river <a href="/wiki/Yamuna_river" class="mw-redirect" title="Yamuna river">Yamuna</a> near the <a href="/wiki/Samadhi_(shrine)" title="Samadhi (shrine)">shrines</a> of his mother Indira Gandhi, brother Sanjay Gandhi, and grandfather Jawaharlal Nehru.<sup id="cite_ref-brit_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Aftermath">Aftermath</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg/220px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="97" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg/330px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg/440px-Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial_lawn.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="704" /></a><figcaption>The <i><a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_Memorial" title="Rajiv Gandhi Memorial">Rajiv Gandhi Memorial</a></i>, at <a href="/wiki/Sriperumbudur" title="Sriperumbudur">Sriperumbudur</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The Supreme Court judgement, by Justice <a href="/wiki/K._T._Thomas_(Justice)" class="mw-redirect" title="K. T. Thomas (Justice)">K. T. Thomas</a>, confirmed that Gandhi was killed because of personal animosity by the LTTE chief <a href="/wiki/Velupillai_Prabhakaran" title="Velupillai Prabhakaran">Prabhakaran</a> arising from his sending the Indian Peace Keeping Force (IPKF) to Sri Lanka and the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force#Controversies" title="Indian Peace Keeping Force">IPKF atrocities</a> against Sri Lankan Tamils.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The Gandhi administration had already antagonised other Tamil militant organisations like <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Liberation_Organisation_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="People&#39;s Liberation Organisation of Tamil Eelam">PLOTE</a> for reversing <a href="/wiki/1988_Maldives_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat_attempt" class="mw-redirect" title="1988 Maldives coup d&#39;état attempt">the 1988 military coup in Maldives</a>. The judgement further cites the death of <a href="/wiki/Thileepan" title="Thileepan">Thileepan</a> in a hunger strike and the suicide by 12 LTTE cadres in a vessel in Oct 1987.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Jain_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Jain Commission">Jain Commission</a> report, various people and agencies are named as suspects in the murder of Rajiv Gandhi. Among them, the cleric <a href="/wiki/Chandraswami" title="Chandraswami">Chandraswami</a> was suspected of involvement, including financing the assassination.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Nalini Sriharan, the only surviving member of the five-member squad behind the assassination of Rajiv Gandhi, is serving life imprisonment. Arrested on 14 June 1991, she and 25 others were sentenced to death by a special court on 28 January 1998. The court confirmed the death sentences of four of the convicts, including Nalini, on 11 May 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Nalini was a close friend of an LTTE operative known as Sriharan alias Murugan, another convict in the case who has been sentenced to death. Nalini later gave birth to a girl, Harithra, in prison. Nalini's death sentence was commuted to life imprisonment in April 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Rajiv's widow, Sonia Gandhi, intervened and asked for clemency for Nalini on the grounds of the latter being a mother.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Later, it was reported that Gandhi's daughter, <a href="/wiki/Priyanka_Gandhi_Vadra" class="mw-redirect" title="Priyanka Gandhi Vadra">Priyanka Gandhi Vadra</a>, had met Nalini at Vellore Central Prison in March 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Nalini regrets the killing of Gandhi and said the real conspirators have not been caught yet.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In August 2011, the president of India rejected the clemency pleas of Murugan and two others on death row—Suthendraraja, alias Santhan, and Perarivalan, alias Arivu.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The execution of the three convicts was scheduled for 9 September 2011. However, the <a href="/wiki/Madras_High_Court" title="Madras High Court">Madras High Court</a> intervened and stayed their executions for eight weeks based on their petitions. In 2010, Nalini had petitioned the Madras High Court seeking release because she had served more than 20 years in prison. She argued that even life convicts were released after 14 years. The state government rejected her request.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Murugan, Santhan and Perarivalan have said they are political prisoners rather than ordinary criminals.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> On 18 February 2014, the Supreme Court of India commuted the death sentences of Murugan, Santhan and Perarivalan to life imprisonment, holding that the 11-year-long delay in deciding their mercy petition had a dehumanising effect on them.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> On 19 February 2014 Tamil Nadu government decided to release all seven convicts in Rajiv Gandhi's assassination case, including A. G. Perarivalan and Nalini.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_India" title="Government of India">Government of India</a> challenged this decision before the Supreme Court, which referred the case to a Constitution Bench.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The report of the Jain Commission created controversy when it accused the <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a> chief minister <a href="/wiki/Karunanidhi" class="mw-redirect" title="Karunanidhi">Karunanidhi</a> of a role in the assassination, leading to Congress withdrawing its support for the <a href="/wiki/I._K._Gujral" class="mw-redirect" title="I. K. Gujral">I. K. Gujral</a> government and fresh elections in 1998. LTTE spokesman <a href="/wiki/Anton_Balasingham" title="Anton Balasingham">Anton Balasingham</a> told the Indian television channel <a href="/wiki/NDTV" title="NDTV">NDTV</a> the killing was a "great tragedy, a monumental historical tragedy which we deeply regret".<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> A memorial called <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Vir Bhumi</a> was constructed at the place of Gandhi's cremation in Delhi. In 1992, the <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_National_Sadbhavana_Award" title="Rajiv Gandhi National Sadbhavana Award">Rajiv Gandhi National Sadbhavana Award</a> was instituted by the Indian National Congress Party.
</p><p>Since his death, 21 May has been declared Anti-Terrorism Day in India.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tnone" style="margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto;overflow:hidden;width:auto;max-width:698px"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="noresize" style="overflow:auto"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="A Panoramic view of Rajiv Gandhi Memorial"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg/690px-Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg" decoding="async" width="690" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg/1035px-Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg/1380px-Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg 2x" data-file-width="10519" data-file-height="3590" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rajib_Gandhi_Memorial.jpg" title="File:Rajib Gandhi Memorial.jpg"> </a></div>A Panoramic view of Rajiv Gandhi Memorial</div></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Institutions_named_after_Gandhi">Institutions named after Gandhi</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_things_named_after_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="List of things named after Rajiv Gandhi">List of things named after Rajiv Gandhi</a></div>
<p>A <a href="/wiki/Right_to_Information" class="mw-redirect" title="Right to Information">Right to Information</a> (RTI) request filed in August 2009 found that more than 450 government projects and schemes are named after the <a href="/wiki/Gandhi-Nehru_family" class="mw-redirect" title="Gandhi-Nehru family">Gandhi-Nehru family</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> In May 2012, <i><a href="/wiki/Zee_News" title="Zee News">Zee News</a></i> reported there were 16 government schemes named after Gandhi, including <i><a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Awas_Yojana" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajiv Awas Yojana">Rajiv Awas Yojana</a></i> and Rajiv Gandhi Udyami Mitra Yojana.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> In March 2015, <a href="/wiki/Haryana" title="Haryana">Haryana</a> sports minister <a href="/wiki/Anil_Vij" title="Anil Vij">Anil Vij</a> said that at that time there were 232 rural stadia in India, with 226 of them being named after him. He said the government was "planning to rename" all the stadia in Haryana state named after him. Vij drew criticism from Congress leader <a href="/wiki/Kuldeep_Sharma" title="Kuldeep Sharma">Kuldeep Sharma</a>, who said it was an "insult to their national leaders".<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Category:Cultural_depictions_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Category:Cultural depictions of Rajiv Gandhi">Category:Cultural depictions of Rajiv Gandhi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Category:Works_about_the_assassination_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Category:Works about the assassination of Rajiv Gandhi">Category:Works about the assassination of Rajiv Gandhi</a></div>
<p>A number of films have been made in India focusing on Rajiv Gandhi's life especially on his assassination. <i>India's Rajiv</i> is a 1991 Indian documentary television series by <a href="/wiki/Simi_Garewal" title="Simi Garewal">Simi Garewal</a>, released closely after Gandhi's assassination it covers his life up to that event.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>  Indian films specifically focusing on the assassination plot include <a href="/wiki/The_Terrorist_(1997_film)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Terrorist (1997 film)"><i>The Terrorist</i></a> (1997) by <a href="/wiki/Santosh_Sivan" title="Santosh Sivan">Santosh Sivan</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cyanide_(2006_film)" title="Cyanide (2006 film)"><i>Cyanide</i></a> (2006) by <a href="/wiki/A._M._R._Ramesh" title="A. M. R. Ramesh">A. M. R. Ramesh</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Kuttrapathirikai" title="Kuttrapathirikai">Kuttrapathirikai</a></i> (2007) by <a href="/wiki/R._K._Selvamani" title="R. K. Selvamani">R. K. Selvamani</a> with <a href="/wiki/Anupam_Kher" title="Anupam Kher">Anupam Kher</a> in the role of Gandhi,<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Mission_90_Days" title="Mission 90 Days">Mission 90 Days</a></i> (2007) by <a href="/wiki/Major_Ravi" title="Major Ravi">Major Ravi</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> and <i><a href="/wiki/Madras_Cafe" title="Madras Cafe">Madras Cafe</a></i> (2013) by <a href="/wiki/Shoojit_Sircar" title="Shoojit Sircar">Shoojit Sircar</a> starring Sanjay Gurbaxani as the former prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Pradhanmantri" title="Pradhanmantri">Pradhanmantri</a></i> (<abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Prime Minister&#39;), a 2013 Indian documentary television series which aired on <a href="/wiki/ABP_News" title="ABP News">ABP News</a> and covers the various policies and political tenures of Indian PMs, includes the tenureship of Rajiv Gandhi in the episodes "Rajiv Gandhi becomes PM and Shah Bano case", "Ayodhya dispute", "Rajiv Gandhi and Bofors scandal", and "Rise of LTTE and Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi" with <a href="/wiki/Mohit_Chauhan_(actor)" title="Mohit Chauhan (actor)">Mohit Chauhan</a> portraying the role of Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
